# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-01

Today’s full expanded search supplies 53 detailed guides, including 27 completed from earlier screened entries, plus 18 additional eligible screened leads. It adds 44 distinct tools to the cumulative library. The plan spans 79 fields, 657 repository queries and 18 model tasks; new adjacencies include printmaking and museum/exhibition media. Live primary-source research supplements every field. New guides cover agent-authored media, neural timelines, audio, CAD, game creation and diagram/print graphics. Open integrations and skills are distinguished from proprietary hosts, paid inference and separately licensed weights/assets. Current setup failures and incomplete production routes remain explicit. No new entry earned Recommended or Ready to try, and no discovered app was installed or run. First guide means new to this library, not a launch today. All platforms were searched equally; actual documented support remains tool-specific. The full existing weekly library review was complete before collection, and each addition receives a current six-check source assessment. Raw unscreened candidates remain local and discovery is not an exhaustive census. Verified collection: 657/657 repository queries attempted; 656 successful, 1 partial, 0 failed and 122 bounded/truncated. All 18 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Separate from successful search queries, 17 watchlist fetches and 1 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Search and review counts

79 creative fields; 657/657 repository queries attempted; 18/18 model-task queries attempted. 28503 distinct source candidates and 862 model leads. 53 detailed profiles and 18 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 1 partial repository queries, 122 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 2 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: OpenSlides’ current primary site and linked GitLab source were read after a direct HTTP failure; Studio/CLI and not-yet-public MCP components were distinguished. A separate Crysto AI README only proposes a possible MIT license and includes placeholder audio, so no new qualifying tool was established. [Source](https://openslides.dev/) [Source](https://gitlab.com/open-slides/openslides) [Source](https://gitlab.com/saqib99/crysto-ai)
- **codeberg** · gap: Live discovery returned a robots/access denial. No eligible Codeberg project was inferred from inaccessible results. [Source](https://codeberg.org/)
- **sourcehut** · gap: Live discovery returned a robots/access denial. This explicit gap is not a claim that the host has no creative AI tools. [Source](https://sr.ht/)
- **packages** · searched: The primary PyPI obs-mcp release page documents 0.2.0, Python 3.10+ and publication provenance. npm haptics.md returned a cache-miss failure and npm tmdlang returned 403; those package-access gaps remain unresolved, supplemented by primary source documentation. [Source](https://pypi.org/project/obs-mcp/) [Source](https://www.npmjs.com/package/haptics.md) [Source](https://www.npmjs.com/package/tmdlang)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Current HappyLatent and MOSS-YXBOX primary documentation describes implemented ComfyUI nodes, model prerequisites and installation routes. Direct registry access was unsuccessful; this is a repository-documentation review rather than a claim that the complete registry was searched. [Source](https://github.com/EricFillion/happylatent) [Source](https://github.com/liangci-ai/MOSS-YXBOX) [Source](https://registry.comfy.org/)
- **project-sites** · searched: Live primary-source fetches cover all 79 observed fields, with PDF web supplements, limited extracts and failed URLs preserved. A primary page is discovery context, not automatic software-license qualification. [Source](https://stitchfoundry.app/) [Source](https://www.imprimatur.fyi/) [Source](https://tmdlang.github.io/Tmd-TS/)
- **research-code** · searched: StuPASE’s primary code/model card, VideoNeuMat’s non-commercial source grant and BCF6’s independent reproduction were researched. Research examples, software grants, model terms and installation reliability were assessed separately. [Source](https://github.com/cisco-open/pase) [Source](https://huggingface.co/cisco-ai/stupase) [Source](https://github.com/bowenxueai/videoneumatcode) [Source](https://github.com/zhr123321a/bcf6)
- **international** · searched: Current Indonesian, Japanese, Chinese and Thai primary documentation was reviewed for Klipsa, zumen, MOSS-YXBOX/TMD-TS and ViralCraft. A source language or research origin does not determine quality or platform compatibility. [Source](https://github.com/mhanafi09051998/klipsa) [Source](https://github.com/meta-taro/zumen) [Source](https://github.com/liangci-ai/MOSS-YXBOX) [Source](https://github.com/bankThanabat/ViralCraft)

## Video Podcast Maker

Video, animation & film · Audio, music & voice · Creative publishing & presentation · AI agents for code-authored media production

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Narrated knowledge videos and animated explainers with an audio-led timeline, repeatable per-video assets and Chinese or English narration.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-01. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The README links publisher examples and describes multi-platform output. These examples were documented, not independently evaluated for factual accuracy, visual quality or platform acceptance. [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/blob/main/skills/video-podcast-maker/SKILL.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Install the full video-podcast-maker skill, or clone the repository. Prepare Python dependencies, FFmpeg and a Remotion project before requesting a topic-driven video. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/blob/main/skills/video-podcast-maker/SKILL.md)
- **Maintenance and support · documented:** Current sampled commit 2026-09-14T08:36:03Z: chore: stop syncing to 365-skills, ship the skill from this repo only (#78). Latest sampled release v5.2.1. The latest 10 issue/PR titles and relevant body excerpts were reviewed for blockers, not exhaustively audited. Activity and closed reports alone do not establish sustained support or a shipped fix. [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/commit/33b8078e3e87c9626d39d144b4c901b4ebf16f74) [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/releases/tag/v5.2.1) [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/pull/81) [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/pull/80) [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/issues/79) [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/pull/78) [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/pull/73) [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/pull/72) [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/pull/71) [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/pull/67) [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/pull/70) [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/pull/69)
- **Independent use · unknown:** Current official documentation and the bounded recent issue/PR sample do not establish independent first-hand creative use for this exact selected workflow. This is not a claim that no independent users exist; author examples and bot dependency updates are insufficient. [Source](https://github.com/agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/issues)
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The integration is MIT. Agent/model access, Microsoft speech services and Remotion have separate terms; Edge synthesis is a remote service despite scripts running locally. Agent access/quota and any Azure speech or optional generation services are separate. The default Edge route is described as keyless; local rendering still consumes compute. [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/blob/main/skills/video-podcast-maker/SKILL.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Research and narration can contain errors. Preview and render approvals remain part of the published workflow. Broad publishing targets do not prove automated platform delivery or current acceptance. Maintenance and independent-use research is limited to current primary documentation and a bounded recent support sample; no installation or hands-on test was performed.

### How it uses AI

A coding-agent workflow researches a topic, writes narration, synthesizes speech and authors Remotion scenes before FFmpeg assembles the video. [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/blob/main/skills/video-podcast-maker/SKILL.md)

### Introduction

A coding-agent workflow researches a topic, writes narration, synthesizes speech and authors Remotion scenes before FFmpeg assembles the video. [Source 1](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/blob/main/skills/video-podcast-maker/SKILL.md)

### What it is good for

Narrated knowledge videos and animated explainers with an audio-led timeline, repeatable per-video assets and Chinese or English narration. [Source 1](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker/blob/main/skills/video-podcast-maker/SKILL.md)

### Demo & examples

The README links publisher examples and describes multi-platform output. These examples were documented, not independently evaluated for factual accuracy, visual quality or platform acceptance. ### Install

Install the full video-podcast-maker skill, or clone the repository. Prepare Python dependencies, FFmpeg and a Remotion project before requesting a topic-driven video. ### First project

1. Choose a short topic and language; review the research and narration before synthesis.

- **Software:** Python 3.8+, Node.js 18+, FFmpeg 4.0+, a compatible coding agent, Python requirements and a Remotion/Chromium rendering environment.

An Android slicer exposes a DeepSeek-agent client and embedded geometry bridges; a separate harness can orchestrate photo-to-3D generation and return a mesh to the plate.
```sh
apksigner verify --print-certs TrioSlicer-<version>.apk
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. 1. Import a small model and the correct machine/material settings; inspect the plate and resolved profile.
2. For AI creation, connect the documented external harness, submit an owned image and inspect the returned geometry.
3. Preview every slice and machine setting; the app’s print confirmations do not validate physical strength or fit.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/tomppi/trioslicer/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/tomppi/trioslicer/blob/main/AI_ASSISTANT.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/tomppi/trioslicer/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

- **Hardware:** The APK is about 500 MB; installed/model/output storage and phone RAM minima are not specified. The assistant guide describes an 8 GB GPU host and an observed roughly 5.7 GB VRAM pipeline peak, not a universal minimum.
- **Software:** Android 10+ on arm64-v8a; embedded CuraEngine 5.14.0-alpha.0, PrusaSlicer 3.0.0-alpha11, OrcaSlicer 2.4.2 and Blender 3.6. AI needs a separately operated harness; photo-to-3D additionally uses a GPU host.
- **Platforms:** Android ARM64 only: the source explicitly excludes other native platforms. The agent/harness/GPU hosts are separate machines, not phone-local model inference.

### How it uses AI

An AI-agent authoring format, skills and compiler translate readable haptic descriptors into platform-specific feedback with capability checks. [Source](https://github.com/Bimbi-Dev/haptics.md/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An AI-agent authoring format, skills and compiler translate readable haptic descriptors into platform-specific feedback with capability checks. [Source 1](https://github.com/Bimbi-Dev/haptics.md/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Adding tactile cues to interactive artworks or apps while keeping a readable design intent and explicit degraded fallbacks. [Source 1](https://github.com/Bimbi-Dev/haptics.md/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README includes a complete pull-to-refresh descriptor and generated platform examples. These document compilation, not a measured physical feel or accessible haptic-braille performance. [Source 1](https://github.com/Bimbi-Dev/haptics.md/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use the published CLI to copy a pattern into a target project. Load the supplied authoring references in a compatible agent and compile for the actual device capabilities. [Source 1](https://github.com/Bimbi-Dev/haptics.md/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the published CLI to copy a pattern into a target project. Load the supplied authoring references in a compatible agent and compile for the actual device capabilities.

```sh
npx haptics add pull-to-refresh --platform ios
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Bimbi-Dev/haptics.md/blob/main/README.md)

1. Select an existing intent and describe the interaction the cue should communicate.
2. Ask the agent to author one haptic JSON block with a web fallback and reduced intent.
3. Integrate the target output and test it on the real device, including missing-capability behavior.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Bimbi-Dev/haptics.md/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Node/npm CLI and the chosen native/web project toolchain; exact Node and system-memory minima are not specified in the reviewed README. The compiler-backed MCP server is planned, not shipped.
- **Platforms:** iOS native feedback and Android vibration have different capabilities. Web vibration is documented only for gesture-gated Android Chrome-family browsers, not iOS Safari or desktop. React Native/Flutter support only intent-level cues.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Bimbi-Dev/haptics.md/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Bimbi-Dev/haptics.md/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Code is Apache-2.0; pattern snippets are CC0. Device APIs, agents and any selected model/service remain separate. The license is not a guarantee about third-party haptic patents.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Code is Apache-2.0; pattern snippets are CC0. Device APIs, agents and any selected model/service remain separate. The license is not a guarantee about third-party haptic patents.
- **Cost:** Local authoring/compilation plus any agent/model access and device hardware.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/Bimbi-Dev/haptics.md/blob/main/README.md)

Intensity and sharpness do not survive to the web target, and many Android devices lack amplitude/composition support. No haptic-braille efficacy or universal tactile equivalence was established. [Source 1](https://github.com/Bimbi-Dev/haptics.md/blob/main/README.md)



```sh
/plugin install tutorials@walkcast
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/walkcast/walkcast/blob/main/README.md)

1. Run /tutorials:start inside the owned app repository and provide a safe demo environment.
2. Review the proposed features, scripts and screenshot storyboards before recording.
3. Inspect the produced video and SOP bundle; rerun only changed tutorials or narration lines when the app changes.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/walkcast/walkcast/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The first narrator download is documented as about 1.2 GB. No minimum RAM/VRAM or total video-output storage is specified.
- **Software:** Node.js 22+, Claude Code, FFmpeg and uv. Kokoro can run on CPU; optional Ming-3.0 uses Apple Silicon/MLX with a separate forced aligner.
- **Platforms:** The instructions include a macOS setup example and an Apple Silicon voice option. A validated Windows/Linux execution matrix is not established by this review.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/walkcast/walkcast/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/walkcast/walkcast/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** Own code is MIT. The source identifies Kokoro as Apache-2.0, Ming-3.0 as MIT and demo music as CC BY 4.0; actual voice/assets and renderer terms remain separate checks.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Own code is MIT. The source identifies Kokoro as Apache-2.0, Ming-3.0 as MIT and demo music as CC BY 4.0; actual voice/assets and renderer terms remain separate checks.
- **Cost:** Claude access/quota and local compute/storage; this route does not require a separate cloud voice/video service.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/walkcast/walkcast/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Recording/narration/rendering are local, but Claude still receives the context sent by its host. Browser automation can change app data; the documented workflow uses demo-data reset procedures. English is the best-tested language. [Source 1](https://github.com/walkcast/walkcast/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/walkcast/walkcast)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/walkcast/walkcast/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

### Introduction

A local MCP bridge lets a compatible AI assistant inspect and control OBS scenes, sources, recording and audio pipelines through obs-websocket. [Source 1](https://github.com/xDarkzx/OBS_MCP/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/project/obs-mcp/)

### What it is good for

Preparing a recording scene, inspecting frame-drop symptoms and applying a documented microphone filter chain before a live-media session. [Source 1](https://github.com/xDarkzx/OBS_MCP/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/project/obs-mcp/)

[Source 1](https://github.com/xDarkzx/OBS_MCP/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/project/obs-mcp/)

### Install

Clone or download the source and use the platform installer, or install the source package manually. Enable OBS’s built-in WebSocket server and configure its local connection in the MCP client. [Source 1](https://github.com/xDarkzx/OBS_MCP/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/project/obs-mcp/)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone or download the source and use the platform installer, or install the source package manually. Enable OBS’s built-in WebSocket server and configure its local connection in the MCP client.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/xDarkzx/OBS_MCP.git
```


```sh
pip install -e .
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/xDarkzx/OBS_MCP/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/project/obs-mcp/)

1. Start OBS and enable WebSocket in Tools; configure the actual local host, port and password privately.
2. Begin with read-only scene/input/stat queries and verify names.
3. Try a change in a rehearsal scene and inspect its effect before relying on the agent during recording or a live stream.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/xDarkzx/OBS_MCP/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/project/obs-mcp/)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.10+, OBS Studio 28+ with obs-websocket v5 and an MCP-compatible AI client. Default port is 4455. Encoding hardware remains dependent on OBS settings.
- **Platforms:** Source installer routes cover Windows and macOS/Linux; compatibility of individual OBS backends and AI hosts is separate.

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/xDarkzx/OBS_MCP/blob/master/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/xDarkzx/OBS_MCP/blob/master/README.md) [Source 3](https://pypi.org/project/obs-mcp/)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Bridge code is Apache-2.0. OBS, the AI host/model and optional filters retain their own terms. Local WebSocket operation does not determine what the AI host sends to its provider.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Bridge code is Apache-2.0. OBS, the AI host/model and optional filters retain their own terms. Local WebSocket operation does not determine what the AI host sends to its provider.

### How it uses AI

MediaPipe hand tracking and a trained random-forest gesture classifier map camera-observed gestures to polyphonic notes and expressive synth controls. [Source](https://github.com/idriss-hamza/hand-gesture-instrument/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

MediaPipe hand tracking and a trained random-forest gesture classifier map camera-observed gestures to polyphonic notes and expressive synth controls. [Source 1](https://github.com/idriss-hamza/hand-gesture-instrument/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Camera-based musical sketches, gesture-performance prototypes and guided instrument experiments without a physical keyboard. [Source 1](https://github.com/idriss-hamza/hand-gesture-instrument/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The source documents guided/free/piano/theremin modes and a synthetic simulator. The simulator bypasses real tracking, and publisher accuracy/latency claims are not independent live-performance measurements. [Source 1](https://github.com/idriss-hamza/hand-gesture-instrument/blob/main/README.md)

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/idriss-hamza/hand-gesture-instrument/blob/main/README.md)

3. Adjust hand roles/instrument modes and listen for missed notes, latency or accidental changes before a performance.

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Analysis metrics help diagnose a mix but do not determine musical quality. The agent/model is external, and no model runs merely because the deterministic render command runs. Maintenance and independent-use research is limited to current primary documentation and a bounded recent support sample; no installation or hands-on test was performed.

### How it uses AI

An AI agent writes musical briefs into YAML, MIDI and code-based parts; deterministic synthesis, mixing and analysis let it inspect and revise the resulting audio. [Source](https://github.com/Joe-Heffer/stemma/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An AI agent writes musical briefs into YAML, MIDI and code-based parts; deterministic synthesis, mixing and analysis let it inspect and revise the resulting audio. [Source 1](https://github.com/Joe-Heffer/stemma/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Headless music/sound sketches with versioned text sources, repeatable renders and machine-readable loudness/spectral feedback. [Source 1](https://github.com/Joe-Heffer/stemma/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The repository includes example-sketch and documented output locations for stems, master WAV/FLAC and analysis JSON. These are reproducibility instructions, not an independently judged listening evaluation. [Source 1](https://github.com/Joe-Heffer/stemma/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Prepare uv and GNU Make, clone the project and build the example track. uv obtains Python 3.12+ and dependencies. Add optional external renderers only for the sources used by a track. [Source 1](https://github.com/Joe-Heffer/stemma/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Prepare uv and GNU Make, clone the project and build the example track. uv obtains Python 3.12+ and dependencies. Add optional external renderers only for the sources used by a track.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/Joe-Heffer/stemma.git
```


```sh
make all TRACK=example-sketch
```


```sh
uv run stemma new night-drive --title "Night Drive"
```


```sh
uv run stemma all night-drive
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Joe-Heffer/stemma/blob/main/README.md)

1. Render the example and inspect its analysis and stems.
2. Ask the agent to write a short musical brief into a new track spec and part sources.
3. Listen to the mix as well as reading metrics; revise arrangement, dynamics and timbre before mastering.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Joe-Heffer/stemma/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** uv with Python 3.12+ and GNU Make. The example has a built-in synthesizer; its optional FluidSynth pad is skipped when unavailable. sfizz, Csound, Faust/C++ and LilyPond are optional route-specific tools.
- **Platforms:** Debian/Ubuntu package examples are documented. Exact tested Windows/macOS/native audio support is not established by this guide.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Joe-Heffer/stemma/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Joe-Heffer/stemma/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** Own code is MIT. Optional effects/renderers, soundfonts and samples retain their own licenses; an open MIDI/YAML format does not grant rights to a chosen sound library.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Own code is MIT. Optional effects/renderers, soundfonts and samples retain their own licenses; an open MIDI/YAML format does not grant rights to a chosen sound library.
- **Cost:** Local synthesis/rendering plus agent access and any separately licensed instruments or samples.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/Joe-Heffer/stemma/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Analysis metrics help diagnose a mix but do not determine musical quality. The agent/model is external, and no model runs merely because the deterministic render command runs. [Source 1](https://github.com/Joe-Heffer/stemma/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/Joe-Heffer/stemma)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/Joe-Heffer/stemma/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The model is an optional AI path among many deterministic features. The app is not notarized on macOS, and file-manager integration differs by desktop. This review did not establish the model’s quality or complete licensing chain. Maintenance and independent-use research is limited to current primary documentation and a bounded recent support sample; no installation or hands-on test was performed.

### How it uses AI

A desktop radial conversion/editor utility adds an optional downloaded neural cut-out model for background removal; ordinary format conversion is not its AI feature. [Source](https://github.com/hasanzadee/convertino/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A desktop radial conversion/editor utility adds an optional downloaded neural cut-out model for background removal; ordinary format conversion is not its AI feature. [Source 1](https://github.com/hasanzadee/convertino/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Quick local background cut-outs and image preparation integrated with file-manager drag/drop workflows. [Source 1](https://github.com/hasanzadee/convertino/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README links interface demos and reports a MacBook M4 test. Those are publisher reports, not independent cut-out quality or all-device validation. [Source 1](https://github.com/hasanzadee/convertino/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install the matching official release for Windows, Linux or macOS using its documented per-user route. Add the optional cut-out model with the application’s model command. [Source 1](https://github.com/hasanzadee/convertino/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the matching official release for Windows, Linux or macOS using its documented per-user route. Add the optional cut-out model with the application’s model command.

```sh
convertino model install
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/hasanzadee/convertino/blob/main/README.md)

1. Start with a copy of a representative image and install the cut-out model.
2. Drag the image, open the Tools ring and choose Cut out; inspect fine edges and alpha.
3. Compare the saved result with the source before batching or using it in a composite.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/hasanzadee/convertino/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The model download is documented as 224 MB with a SHA-256 check. System RAM, VRAM and total installed/output storage minima are not specified.
- **Software:** The packaged desktop app plus its optional model. FFmpeg and other helpers are needed only for respective video/audio/document features; their presence does not improve neural cut-out accuracy.
- **Platforms:** Windows 10/11, Linux and macOS 13+ Apple Silicon/Intel are documented. Wayland limits the global drag/trigger gesture; context-menu/clipboard alternatives are documented.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/hasanzadee/convertino/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/hasanzadee/convertino/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** App source is MIT. The selected cut-out model’s full grant and any external helper terms need separate review; the root app license does not settle weight rights.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. App source is MIT. The selected cut-out model’s full grant and any external helper terms need separate review; the root app license does not settle weight rights.
- **Cost:** Local compute/storage; the documented core route does not upload files to an inference service.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/hasanzadee/convertino/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The model is an optional AI path among many deterministic features. The app is not notarized on macOS, and file-manager integration differs by desktop. This review did not establish the model’s quality or complete licensing chain. [Source 1](https://github.com/hasanzadee/convertino/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. This guide scopes the local-file route. Shared-tab capture is explicitly unreliable in current QA, with a reported frozen/silent recording. Sampled scanning is not full transcription; persistent projects, multicamera sync and guaranteed MP4 encoding are outside the build. Maintenance and independent-use research is limited to current primary documentation and a bounded recent support sample; no installation or hands-on test was performed. Open PR #1 proposes a persistent V2 studio, new minimum Node 22.13+, stable playback and actual social-export recipes. Those unmerged changes do not describe the assessed main-branch local-file workflow.

### How it uses AI

A local producer interface samples recording windows and sends selected audio/frames to Meta models for story suggestions, transcription or translation. [Source](https://github.com/SayamDev/context-desk/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A local producer interface samples recording windows and sends selected audio/frames to Meta models for story suggestions, transcription or translation. [Source 1](https://github.com/SayamDev/context-desk/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Finding and refining short story moments in an owned local recording, with editor-controlled captions, crop and exports. [Source 1](https://github.com/SayamDev/context-desk/blob/main/README.md)

The README documents bounded visual passes and actual QA failures in shared-tab capture. These are useful limitations; they do not establish independent clipping quality for long recordings. [Source 1](https://github.com/SayamDev/context-desk/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Prepare Node.js 20+, copy the sample environment privately, configure the Meta Model API key and start the local server. Optional link imports need the pinned importer plus Python/FFmpeg. [Source 1](https://github.com/SayamDev/context-desk/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Prepare Node.js 20+, copy the sample environment privately, configure the Meta Model API key and start the local server. Optional link imports need the pinned importer plus Python/FFmpeg.

```sh
cp .env.example .env
```


```sh
npm run dev
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/SayamDev/context-desk/blob/main/README.md)

1. Upload an owned local recording and review automation settings before scanning.
2. Inspect suggested moments and the evidence windows behind them; edit cuts and captions.
3. Save the video and notes before reloading, and play each export to confirm picture and sound.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/SayamDev/context-desk/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Recordings can occupy many GB and temporary copies; the app documents cache/file budgets but no minimum system RAM, VRAM or free-disk requirement for all workflows.
- **Software:** Node.js 20+ and a current Chromium browser for live capture. Optional importer: Python 3.10+ and FFmpeg. Local sources up to six hours/20 GB are documented with staging/cache limits.
- **Platforms:** Browser/local Node workflow. OS compatibility is not independently verified; capture depends on actual tab-share/audio permissions and background throttling.

- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Application code is MIT. Meta service terms and source-footage rights remain separate. A reachable public recording URL is not permission to download or republish it.

- **Platforms:** Windows is documented; SOLIDWORKS versions other than 2025 are explicitly untested. No macOS/Linux native route is established.

### How it uses AI

### Introduction

An MCP-accessible audio toolchain lets an AI agent audit, repair and master files through measured deterministic DSP operations. [Source 1](https://github.com/The-40-Thieves/audio-forge/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Preparing delivery masters with explicit technical pass/fail reports, and optionally exporting stems, MIDI or a REAPER session. [Source 1](https://github.com/The-40-Thieves/audio-forge/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README documents a delivery report and sample commands. A target-spec pass measures selected technical conditions; it is not a universal streaming-platform acceptance or musical-quality certificate. [Source 1](https://github.com/The-40-Thieves/audio-forge/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Build the Rust CLI or MCP server from source. Configure the server’s allowed music root and connect an MCP client; optional plugin/REAPER paths need their own toolchains. [Source 1](https://github.com/The-40-Thieves/audio-forge/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Build the Rust CLI or MCP server from source. Configure the server’s allowed music root and connect an MCP client; optional plugin/REAPER paths need their own toolchains.

```sh
cargo build --release -p forge-mcp
```


```sh
cargo run --release --bin forge -- deliver mix.wav -o out/master.wav --target spotify
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/The-40-Thieves/audio-forge/blob/main/README.md)

1. Use an owned mix and inspect the input audit before applying repairs.
2. Compare the repaired/mastered output against the original and review loudness/true-peak findings.
3. Listen end to end and validate the actual destination’s needs before delivery.

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/The-40-Thieves/audio-forge/blob/main/README.md)

- **Weights:** Own code is Apache-2.0. Optional analysis/separation/transcription dependencies, plugin SDKs, DAWs and source media retain separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Own code is Apache-2.0. Optional analysis/separation/transcription dependencies, plugin SDKs, DAWs and source media retain separate terms.

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/cisco-open/pase/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/cisco-open/pase/blob/main/stupase/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/cisco-open/pase/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 4](https://github.com/cisco-open/pase/blob/main/LICENSE.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/cisco-open/pase/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 6](https://huggingface.co/cisco-ai/stupase) [Source 7](https://xiaobin-rong.github.io/stupase_demo/)

1. Prepare a short authorized speech recording and the correct subproject/checkpoints.
2. Run the documented inference entry point only after resolving compatible dependencies.
3. Compare enhanced and original speech for changed words, identity and artifacts; preserve the source recording.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/cisco-open/pase/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/cisco-open/pase/blob/main/stupase/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/cisco-open/pase/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 4](https://github.com/cisco-open/pase/blob/main/LICENSE.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/cisco-open/pase/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 6](https://huggingface.co/cisco-ai/stupase) [Source 7](https://xiaobin-rong.github.io/stupase_demo/)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** PyTorch/torchaudio and the listed audio libraries. The current requirements file pins both torch 2.4.1 and 2.1.1, and both torchaudio 2.4.1 and 2.1.1, which conflict. StuPASE’s model card scopes input to 16 kHz mono speech.
- **Platforms:** The inference flag documents CUDA devices and CPU. Native OS support, MPS behavior and real-time operation are not established; training on two RTX 4090s is not an inference minimum.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. - **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Original code is Apache-2.0 with retained third-party MIT/Apache notices. The StuPASE model card declares Apache-2.0 for the released model; dataset terms and other checkpoints remain separate.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Original code is Apache-2.0 with retained third-party MIT/Apache notices. The StuPASE model card declares Apache-2.0 for the released model; dataset terms and other checkpoints remain separate.
- **Cost:** Local speech-inference compute and model storage; no mandatory paid inference API is documented.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. ### How it uses AI

An MCP/skill toolchain lets agents generate or edit geometry through several local engines, inspect validation results and prepare previews/handoffs. ### Install

Install the source with the render extra, run doctor/backends, and add only the required geometry engines. Connect forge3d-mcp to the agent with a dedicated work directory. [Source 1](https://github.com/chaudharyvivek1/forge3d/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/chaudharyvivek1/forge3d/blob/main/docs/licensing.md)

```sh
git clone https://github.com/chaudharyvivek1/forge3d.git
```


```sh
pip install -e ".[render]"
```


```sh
forge3d doctor
```


```sh
forge3d backends
```

### Introduction

An AI agent writes a constrained YAML illustration, reads geometric/composition feedback from a CPU compiler and revises it before SVG/PNG export. [Source 1](https://github.com/guilhermebkel/scenec/blob/main/README.md)

```sh
npx skills add guilhermebkel/scenec -g
```


```sh
npx @guilhermebkel/scenec check scene.yaml
```


```sh
npx @guilhermebkel/scenec render scene.yaml -o out --png
```

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/guilhermebkel/scenec/blob/main/README.md)

2. Review the scene and warnings, and revise the YAML through the agent.
3. Inspect the SVG and PNG at the destination size; a riso appearance is not a physical ink-separation workflow.

Open the official .iinaplgz release in IINA, or package from source. Prepare Xcode command-line tools and a chosen local speech engine/model. The README conflicts on whether whisper-cli is fetched automatically or must be installed with Homebrew; manual engine installation is a documented route. [Source 1](https://github.com/wxmpro/iina-sub/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Open the official .iinaplgz release in IINA, or package from source. Prepare Xcode command-line tools and a chosen local speech engine/model. The README conflicts on whether whisper-cli is fetched automatically or must be installed with Homebrew; manual engine installation is a documented route.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/wxmpro/iina-sub.git
```


```sh
bash scripts/package.sh
```


```sh
open dist/io.github.xinmin.iina-sub.iinaplgz
```


```sh
brew install whisper.cpp
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/wxmpro/iina-sub/blob/main/README.md)

1. Open a small local video and choose the correct speech language/model.
2. Review the early cues, speaker names and chunk-boundary repetitions.
3. Export SRT beside the video and correct text/timing before any accessibility or publishing use.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/wxmpro/iina-sub/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Model files listed at 141 MB (base), 465 MB (small), 514 MB (medium quantized), 547 MB (large-v3-turbo quantized) or 1031 MB (large-v3 quantized). These are model sizes, not total disk/RAM requirements.
- **Software:** macOS 13+, IINA 1.3+, Xcode command-line tools, whisper-cli or another configured supported local engine and model. Exact CPU/RAM minima are not stated.
- **Platforms:** macOS/IINA only in this documented route. An M1 Pro timing is supplied; it does not establish a tested Intel/Apple-Silicon matrix for every engine.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/wxmpro/iina-sub/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/wxmpro/iina-sub/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** Plugin source is MIT. IINA, Whisper engines, downloaded model distributions and any custom engine retain separate grants. The full current grant for every selectable model was not independently reviewed here.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Plugin source is MIT. IINA, Whisper engines, downloaded model distributions and any custom engine retain separate grants. The full current grant for every selectable model was not independently reviewed here.
- **Cost:** Local compute/model storage; no cloud transcription key is required for the documented local engine.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/wxmpro/iina-sub/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The source contradicts itself about automatic engine downloads and first-run behavior. Models can omit fillers or mishear language/names; chunk boundaries may repeat words. A subtitle export does not certify accessibility, and local processing claims do not replace a security audit. [Source 1](https://github.com/wxmpro/iina-sub/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/wxmpro/iina-sub)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/wxmpro/iina-sub/blob/main/README.md)

## Altocue

Video, animation & film · Audio, music & voice · Editing, captions & post-production · AI agents for code-authored media production

The repository was created on 2026-10-31. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Turning short talking-head footage into inspectable edits with editable files, technical QA and explicit human review.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-01. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The README links a raw/edited demo whose presenter is AI-generated and mistakes are planted. The edit is an author example; neither that demo nor an Editor-in-Chief model score establishes independent creative quality. [Source](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/LICENSES.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Install the source package with uv, prepare FFmpeg, then review the optional renderer/denoiser setup prompts. Motion graphics need Node 22+; Linux picture-text QA also needs Tesseract. Connect the skills to the chosen agent. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/LICENSES.md)
- **Maintenance and support · documented:** Current sampled commit 2026-10-31T23:48:01Z: Lead stock search with Pixabay, because Pexels has paused new API keys. Latest sampled release v0.1.0. The latest 0 issue/PR titles and relevant body excerpts were reviewed for blockers, not exhaustively audited. Activity and closed reports alone do not establish sustained support or a shipped fix. [Source](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/commit/8aeb6f89a68b0e0ed494a620020b7f25d5cd494c) [Source](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/releases/tag/v0.1.0)
- **Independent use · unknown:** Current official documentation and the bounded recent issue/PR sample do not establish independent first-hand creative use for this exact selected workflow. This is not a claim that no independent users exist; author examples and bot dependency updates are insufficient. [Source](https://github.com/stratoshouse/altocue/issues)
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Own source is MIT. The complete license inventory separates fonts, model grants, FFmpeg builds and optional HyperFrames. HyperFrames bundles non-open-source GSAP even though Altocue says its graphics do not call it; Chrome and external AI hosts/services have separate terms. Local compute plus the chosen coding agent. Hosted generation/review is optional and billed separately on the user’s own key. [Source](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/LICENSES.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Static per-shot crops cannot follow a walking subject. Heavy echo/clipping cannot be repaired reliably. The source says fal.ai, Gemini, stock search and the Higgsfield CLI route were tested against fake servers, not established live. A fresh agent reviewer is not independent first-hand user evidence. Maintenance and independent-use research is limited to current primary documentation and a bounded recent support sample; no installation or hands-on test was performed.

### How it uses AI

An agent editing suite combines local neural transcription/denoising with deterministic cuts, captions, graphics and a separate model-assisted review pack. [Source](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/LICENSES.md)

### Introduction

An agent editing suite combines local neural transcription/denoising with deterministic cuts, captions, graphics and a separate model-assisted review pack. [Source 1](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/LICENSES.md)

### What it is good for

Turning short talking-head footage into inspectable edits with editable files, technical QA and explicit human review. [Source 1](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/LICENSES.md)

### Demo & examples

The README links a raw/edited demo whose presenter is AI-generated and mistakes are planted. The edit is an author example; neither that demo nor an Editor-in-Chief model score establishes independent creative quality. [Source 1](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/LICENSES.md)

### Install

Install the source package with uv, prepare FFmpeg, then review the optional renderer/denoiser setup prompts. Motion graphics need Node 22+; Linux picture-text QA also needs Tesseract. Connect the skills to the chosen agent. [Source 1](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/LICENSES.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the source package with uv, prepare FFmpeg, then review the optional renderer/denoiser setup prompts. Motion graphics need Node 22+; Linux picture-text QA also needs Tesseract. Connect the skills to the chosen agent.

```sh
uv tool install git+https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue
```


```sh
altocue setup
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/LICENSES.md)

1. Give the agent a short authorized source clip, brief and output format.
2. Inspect transcript, proposed cuts, reframing and captions before final rendering.
3. Review the full video and technical gates; treat model review as a separate fallible opinion and approve manually.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/LICENSES.md)

- **Hardware:** Source suggests 16 GB memory as comfortable and about 3.5 GB disk, including roughly 2.5 GB speech model; transcription uses about 4 GB memory. These are documented estimates, not verified minimums.
- **Software:** uv and FFmpeg; Node.js 22+ for motion graphics; Tesseract for Linux picture-text QA. Apple Silicon uses parakeet-mlx; elsewhere the source uses faster-whisper. Optional DeepFilterNet, HyperFrames and Chrome add dependencies.
- **Platforms:** Apple Silicon and Linux dependency paths are documented; a complete independently reproduced OS matrix is not established. Hosted provider paths vary in live-test depth.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/LICENSES.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** Own source is MIT. The complete license inventory separates fonts, model grants, FFmpeg builds and optional HyperFrames. HyperFrames bundles non-open-source GSAP even though Altocue says its graphics do not call it; Chrome and external AI hosts/services have separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Own source is MIT. The complete license inventory separates fonts, model grants, FFmpeg builds and optional HyperFrames. HyperFrames bundles non-open-source GSAP even though Altocue says its graphics do not call it; Chrome and external AI hosts/services have separate terms.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus the chosen coding agent. Hosted generation/review is optional and billed separately on the user’s own key.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/StratosHouse/altocue/blob/main/LICENSES.md)

### Limitations

Static per-shot crops cannot follow a walking subject. Heavy echo/clipping cannot be repaired reliably. The source says fal.ai, Gemini, stock search and the Higgsfield CLI route were tested against fake servers, not established live. A fresh agent reviewer is not independent first-hand user evidence. ### BCF6 independent reproduction · MIT

Researching compressed neural materials and reproducible training/storage trade-offs for graphics.
A learned material representation is trained into block-compressed features with an MLP decoder, BC6H export and independent reload evaluation.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. MIT own implementation; datasets and upstream decoder components retain separate terms. Linux CUDA/PyTorch 2.7.1+cu118 and GCC 11.4 are the reported environment, not a general minimum. Windows acceptance and hardware shader integration are pending; full setup/hardware guide remains pending.

### NeuralPass · MIT

Offline-game experiments in temporally persistent style transfer and material texture baking.
A ReShade add-on uses neural frame restyling with temporal rejection and persistent UV atlases, including SD-Turbo/StreamDiffusion and ONNX/DirectML routes.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. MIT project code; ReShade, inference runtimes and downloaded model grants are separate. The unsigned Windows preview needs full add-on support; the source explicitly excludes protected/anti-cheat multiplayer use. Exact setup, model terms and GPU limits need a complete guide.

### AI Film Pipeline — reference scripts · MIT

Studying measured shot, character-consistency and sound-sync workflows on an 8 GB NVIDIA card.
Local reference scripts coordinate Z-Image/LoRA keyframes, Wan 2.2 animation, SeedVR2 upscaling and frame-spotted sound for a film production.

They target the author’s own Windows/ComfyUI portable folders and shot lists and are explicitly not turnkey. Reported RTX 4060 Ti 8 GB timings are bounded author measurements. Models, fan-film IP, library sounds and training data require separate checks. The author excludes non-commercial MMAudio/AudioLDM2 outputs from published films; that is a publisher policy, not a completed rights audit.

Open agent skills connect an AI host to a remote service for AI instrument rendering, timbre transfer, stems and scoring-to-picture jobs.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. MIT applies to these skills, not to the remote doseedo inference service or its models. Account sign-in and priced jobs are required; no local model implementation is supplied. The service says the agent writes notes and doseedo plays them, rather than composing from a text prompt. OS-specific desktop/DAW, model and pricing details need a full guide.

### MotionBricks Unity Bridge · Apache-2.0

Generating short character motion segments for prototyping, with offline-baked clips as a mobile-game handoff.
An inference-server bridge maps learned MotionBricks SOMA motion into Unity Humanoid playback or editor-baked animation clips.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Apache-2.0 bridge code; upstream MotionBricks code and NVIDIA Open Model License weights are separate, with full weight-term review pending. Server docs require NVIDIA/CUDA 12/TensorRT/Python 3.12; client Unity 2021.3+. Retargeting is approximate, motion is kinematic and baked clips are generic transform clips, not Humanoid muscle-space. Author GPU checks do not prove physical feasibility or arbitrary-character compatibility.

### Ondina DAW · MIT

Agent-assisted music/audio sessions with native editable project state.
A Windows Rust DAW offers a local-model assistant and MCP control over composition, arrangement, audio editing and rendering.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. MIT own code; llama.cpp/GGUF models, neural splitter, VST plugins and FFmpeg remain separate. Physical MIDI keyboard and proprietary Atmos import are untested; score view is display-only.

### CyberArm hand-tracking control · MIT

Researching gesture-controlled physical interaction and teleoperated creative installations.
MediaPipe hand tracking provides a vision-control route for a wireless five-DOF arm, alongside a Three.js digital twin and haptic glove.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. MIT project source; MediaPipe models/libraries and hardware retain separate terms. ESP32-C3 glove, ESP32 arm controller, sensors, servos, Arduino core 3+ and WebSockets library are named. The documented latency/interlocks are publisher claims; no physical safety or reliable art-production validation was established. Complete wiring, power, calibration and code-route review remains pending.

### briefcut · MIT

Inspectable short films with explicit shot, timing, provenance and review artifacts.
An agent turns a Markdown film brief into model-generated assets, then deterministic code assembles frame-synced sound/video and technical QA.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. MIT own pipeline; external agents, video/audio services, weights and supplied media have separate terms. Node 22+ and FFmpeg are documented. The one-command demo uses mock test-pattern clips and tones, not live AI generation; publisher finished-film examples and mock QA must be distinguished. Full provider and OS guide remains pending.

### Rosada Short-form Editor · MIT

Profile-driven vertical edits with inspectable caption, sound and technical-check outputs.
A talking-head pipeline combines speech transcription and agent-authored style/edit recipes with optional generated audio and local rendering.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. MIT code; speech model, fonts, ElevenLabs and input-media rights remain separate. Python 3, FFmpeg/ffprobe with libass/libx264, DejaVu/fonts and optional Chrome are named. Sound can fall back to procedural synthesis; that fallback is not neural generation. A passing technical report does not establish truthful captions, creative quality or platform acceptance.

### Comic Factory · MIT

Human-reviewed short comics with distinct balloon and voice passes.
A local Windows workflow coordinates a writer, image generator, visual reviewer and speech model into lettered comics and voiced still-image videos.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. MIT app; model, font, CUDA/runtime and demo-character rights are separate. Beta targets Windows x64, NVIDIA Ampere+, 8 GB VRAM, 24 GB RAM (32 recommended), about 20 GB downloads and 60 GB free storage plus projects. Source says clean-PC/full-generation testing is still outstanding. Demo fan art is not cleared by the app license, and videos are still imagery rather than animated characters. An early-beta label or missing independent clean-PC test alone is not an Experimental verdict.

## Excluded and unresolved findings

Research notes only; these do not enter the eligible tool library.

- **RoboExhibit license conflict** · needs-license-review: The current README calls the project GPLv3, while the complete root software license is Apache-2.0. Its concrete LLM/Unity museum-guide route is promising, but the conflicting software-license identity and separately hosted Unity frontend need clarification before inclusion. [Source](https://github.com/ashenclock/ROBOEXHIBIT)
- **SMV Viewer Prototype** · excluded: The complete root license explicitly covers the page, JavaScript and compressed scenes with CC BY-NC-ND 4.0. Its non-commercial/no-derivatives conditions do not qualify as open-source software. Separate decoder/vendor grants do not relicense the whole player. [Source](https://github.com/miya9756/smv-viewer-prototype/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Dramai license identity** · needs-license-review: The complete root license deletes wording from Apache Section 6 and changes Section 9 liability wording to support. The recognized Apache label does not settle this modified grant; clarification is required before a new eligible guide. [Source](https://github.com/hyyyyyyz/dramai/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **eian-riso-broll** · excluded: The full software grant restricts use to personal/non-commercial contexts and prohibits redistribution; organization/commercial use needs a separate grant. Popularity and printmaking relevance do not remove those terms. [Source](https://github.com/YIAN6557/eian-riso-broll/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Infinode** · excluded: The current complete software license is PolyForm Noncommercial; its creative workflow does not meet this project’s open-source software policy. [Source](https://github.com/xingchen-mu/infinode/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **AgenticCAD current software** · excluded: The current software grant is PolyForm Noncommercial, rather than a qualifying open-source grant. [Source](https://github.com/agenticcad/agenticcad/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **VideoNeuMat code** · excluded: The original software is licensed CC BY-NC 4.0. A separately Apache-licensed dependency does not relicense this non-commercial project code. [Source](https://github.com/bowenxueai/videoneumatcode/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **LyriFont** · needs-license-review: The primary page establishes a creative AI typography workflow, but a complete qualifying software license was not established in the inspected repository. [Source](https://github.com/moeni27/LyriFont)
- **Museum AI candidates** · needs-license-review: Museum semantic-search and exhibition candidates were researched, but complete qualifying grants were not established and the proposed semantic-search demo URL returned 404. They were not counted as eligible leads. [Source](https://github.com/derekphilipau/museum-semantic-search)
- **EmbroideryAI-Research current release** · excluded: The README explicitly says there is no image-to-embroidery ML model or working GUI agent. Its current deterministic codec research does not establish an implemented creative AI workflow. [Source](https://github.com/asadabumba/EmbroideryAI-Research)
- **PoseForge current implementation** · excluded: The reviewed current route provides deterministic proxy geometry; the described model/agent integration is planned. AI-art subject matter alone is insufficient. [Source](https://github.com/beatweapon/PoseForge)
- **AI Foley SFX Generator implementation** · needs-license-review: The download-oriented README makes broad claims but this pass did not establish a verifiable open implementation and model/source chain. Its metadata grant alone does not qualify the advertised executable. [Source](https://github.com/BodyPerfumerCabin86/ai-foley-sfx-generator)
- **OpenSuno-YuE2 current stack** · excluded: The complete third-party notice assigns substantial implementation files as well as weights to non-commercial terms. Its MIT integration does not make the whole current stack open source. Existing historical entries and warnings are preserved. [Source](https://github.com/victorcampeanu/OpenSuno-YuE2/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)
- **Varen current binary** · excluded: The current repository describes a private product/release portal with removed source. The existing library’s current-license warning is preserved; the historical AGPL entry does not qualify this new binary. [Source](https://github.com/vanyu0710/Varen-AI-CAD)
- **Palmier Pro current binary** · excluded: The source distinguishes historical GPL code through 0.7.6 from later proprietary binaries. Current proprietary releases are not newly presented as open source. [Source](https://github.com/palmier-io/palmier-pro)
